arr[valid[nearest]]\n\n            vx = np.gradient(x) * VIDEO_FR\n            vy = np.gradient(y) * VIDEO_FR\n            speeds.append(np.sqrt(vx**2 + vy**2))\n\n        # Average across paw features\n        avg_speed = np.mean(speeds, axis=0)\n\n        try:\n            ft = np.array(traj_bottom['frameTimes'][trix]).flatten()\n            old_time = ft - vidshift - align_times[trix]\n        except:\n            nframes = ts.shape[0]\n            ft = np.arange(1, nframes + 1) / VIDEO_FR\n            old_time = ft - 0.5 - align_times[trix]\n\n        if len(old_time) > 1:\n            f_interp = interp1d(old_time, avg_speed, kind='linear',\n                               bounds_error=False, fill_value=np.nan)\n            paw_vel[:, trix] = f_interp(time_axis)\n\n    # Fill NaN\n    for trix in range(ntrials):\n        col = paw_vel[:, trix]\n        nans = np.isnan(col)\n        if nans.any() and not nans.all():\n            valid_idx = np.where(~nans)[0]\n            nan_idx = np.where(nans)[0]\n            nearest = np.searchsorted(valid_idx, nan_idx).clip(0, len(valid_idx)-1)\n            col[nans] = col[valid_idx[nearest]]\n\n    return paw_vel\n\n\ndef discretize_per_session(data_2d, percentile=50):\n    \"\"\"Discretize time-varying data using per-session threshold.\n    data_2d: (n_time, n_trials)\n    Returns: (n_time, n_trials) binary array (0: < threshold, 1: >= threshold)\n    \"\"\"\n    all_vals = data_2d[~np.isnan(data_2d)]\n    if len(all_vals) == 0:\n        return np.zeros_like(data_2d, dtype=np.int32)\n    threshold = np.percentile(all_vals, percentile)\n    # If threshold is 0 (e.g. speed data with many zeros), use a tiny positive value\n    # so that exact zeros become \"low\" and any positive value is \"high\"\n    if threshold == 0:\n        threshold = np.finfo(np.float32).eps\n    result = (data_2d >= threshold).astype(np.int32)\n    # Replace any remaining NaN positions with 0\n    result[np.isnan(data_2d)] = 0\n    return result\n\n\n# ============================================================\n# MAIN PROCESSING\n# ============================================================\n\ndef process_session(anm, date, probes, data_dir, show_processing=False, session_idx=0):\n    \"\"\"Process a single session and return formatted data.\"\"\"\n\n    t_start = time.time()\n\n    # Load data\n    obj, me_raw, me_thresh, t_load = load_session_data(anm, date, data_dir)\n    print(f\"  Loaded in {t_load:.1f}s\")\n\n    bp = obj['bp']\n    ntrials_total = int(bp['Ntrials'])\n\n    # Get event times\n    ev = bp['ev']\n    goCue = np.array(ev['goCue']).flatten()\n\n    # Get trial masks\n    R = np.array(bp['R']).flatten().astype(bool)\n    L = np.array(bp['L']).flatten().astype(bool)\n    hit = np.array(bp['hit']).flatten().astype(bool)\n    miss = np.array(bp['miss']).flatten().astype(bool)\n    no = np.array(bp['no']).flatten().astype(bool)\n    autowater = np.array(bp['autowater']).flatten().astype(bool)\n    early = np.array(bp['early']).flatten().astype(bool)\n\n    stim_enable = np.zeros(ntrials_total, dtype=bool)\n    stim = bp.get('stim')\n    if stim is not None and isinstance(stim, dict):\n        se = stim.get('enable')\n        if se is not None:\n            stim_enable = np.array(se).flatten().astype(bool)\n\n    # Trial selection: exclude early, stim, and no-response (ignore) trials\n    valid_mask = ~early & ~stim_enable & ~no\n    # Also need a lick response (hit or miss)\n    valid_mask = valid_mask & (hit | miss)\n\n    valid_trials = np.where(valid_mask)[0]  # 0-indexed\n    n_valid = len(valid_trials)\n\n    if n_valid < 2:\n        print(f\"  WARNING: Only {n_valid} valid trials, skipping session\")\n        return None\n\n    print(f\"  Trials: {ntrials_total} total, {n_valid} valid ({n_valid/ntrials_total*100:.0f}%)\")\n\n    # Compute video offset\n    vidshift = compute_video_offset(obj)\n\n    # Get alignment times\n    align_times_all = goCue  # for all trials\n\n    # Process neural data from specified probes\n    all_cluster_indices = []\n    clu_data = obj['clu']\n\n    for probe_num in probes:\n        probe_idx = probe_num - 1  # 0-indexed\n        clu_probe = clu_data[probe_idx]\n\n        if clu_probe is None:\n            continue\n\n        if isinstance(clu_probe, dict):\n            valid_clu = get_valid_cluster_indices(clu_probe)\n            all_cluster_indices.append((probe_idx, valid_clu))\n            print(f\"  Probe {probe_num}: {len(clu_probe['quality'])} total, {len(valid_clu)} valid clusters\")\n\n    # Bin and smooth spikes for all valid clusters across probes\n    t_bin_start = time.time()\n    edges = np.arange(TMIN, TMAX + DT, DT)\n    time_axis = edges[:-1] + DT / 2\n    n_time = len(time_axis)\n\n    # Count total neurons\n    total_neurons = sum(len(v) for _, v in all_cluster_indices)\n\n    # Process spikes for all neurons at once\n    trialdat = np.zeros((n_time, total_neurons, ntrials_total), dtype=np.float32)\n\n    neuron_offset = 0\n    for probe_idx, valid_clu in all_cluster_indices:\n        clu_probe = clu_data[probe_idx]\n\n        for i, clu_idx in enumerate(valid_clu):\n            trial_arr = np.array(clu_probe['trial'][clu_idx]).flatten()\n            trialtm_arr = np.array(clu_probe['trialtm'][clu_idx]).flatten()\n\n            for j in range(ntrials_total):\n                trial_num = j + 1\n                spk_mask = trial_arr == trial_num\n                if not np.any(spk_mask):\n                    continue\n\n                aligned = trialtm_arr[spk_mask] - align_times_all[j]\n                counts, _ = np.histogram(aligned, bins=edges)\n                fr = counts.astype(np.float32) / DT\n                trialdat[:, neuron_offset + i, j] = causal_gaussian_smooth(fr, SMOOTH_WINDOW, BC_TYPE)\n\n        neuron_offset += len(valid_clu)\n\n    t_bin = time.time() - t_bin_start\n    print(f\"  Spike binning: {t_bin:.1f}s for {total_neurons} {n_neurons_final} neurons (removed {total_neurons - n_neurons_final})\")\n\n    if n_neurons_final < 10:\n        print(f\"  WARNING: Only {n_neurons_final} neurons after filtering, skipping session\")\n        return None\n\n    # Extract valid trials only\n    trialdat_valid = trialdat[:, :, valid_trials]  # (n_time, n_neurons, n_valid)\n\n    # Process behavioral outputs\n    # 1. Build trial-level data structures\n    neural_trials = []\n    input_trials = []\n    output_trials = []\n\n    for t_idx in range(n_valid):\n        # Neural: (n_neurons, n_time)\n        neural_trials.append(trialdat_valid[:, :, t_idx].T.astype(np.float32))\n\n        # Input: time from goCue in seconds (1, n_time)\n        input_trials.append(time_axis.reshape(1, -1).astype(np.float32))\n\n        # Output: (n_output, n_time) for time-varying, (n_output,) for per-trial\n        # Outputs 0-2 are per-trial, 3-5 are time-varying\n        out = np.zeros((6, n_time), dtype=np.int32)\n        out[0, :] = lick_direction[t_idx]  # broadcast per-trial to time\n        out[1, :] = context[t_idx]\n        out[2, :] = outcome[t_idx]\n        out[3, :] = tongue_vel_disc[:, t_idx]\n        out[4, :] = paw_vel_disc[:, t_idx]\n        out[5, :] = me_disc[:, t_idx]\n        output_trials.append(out)\n\n    # Remove clusters with mean FR < lowFR\n    60→\n    61→### Video/Motion Energy Processing\n    62→- Video at 400 Hz\n    63→- Video offset: `vidshift = mode(sglx.bitcode.bitstart)/sglx.fs - Remove clusters with mean FR < lowFR\n\n### Video/Motion Energy Processing\n- Video at 400 Hz\n- Video offset: `vidshift = mode(sglx.bitcode.bitstart)/sglx.fs - mode(bp.ev.bitStart)`\n- Motion energy PCA\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Data Structure\n- Each session stored as `data_structure_ANM_DATE.mat` (HDF5/MATLAB v7.3)\n- Loaded with `mat73.loadmat()` -> dict with key `obj`\n- `obj.bp` - behavioral data (Ntrials, R, L, hit, miss, no, autowater, early, stim, ev)\n- `obj.clu` - list of probes; each probe is dict with keys: quality, site, tm, trial, trialtm, spkWavs\n- `obj.traj` - list of 2 views (side, bottom); each is dict with frameTimes, ts, featNames per trial\n- `obj.ex` - session metadata (anm, day, probe locations)\n- `obj.sglx` - spike Same approach as the reference.\n\n## 4-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Lick direction* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from `bp.R`, `bp.L`, `bp.hit`, and `bp.miss`. The combination of instructed side and outcome determines lick direction.\n\nii.\n```python\nR = np.array(bp['R']).flatten().astype(bool)\nL = np.array(bp['L']).flatten().astype(bool)\nhit = np.array(bp['hit']).flatten().astype(bool)\nmiss = np.array(bp['miss']).flatten().astype(bool)\n```\n\niii. A hit on the instructed side means the animal licked that side; a miss means it licked the opposite side. The AI computes: `lick_right = (R & hit) | (L & miss)`, then converts to int (0=left, 1=right). Since ignore trials are excluded, there are only 2 classes. The instructions specify \"left, right, none\" as the output values.\n\n## 5-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Behavioral context* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from `bp.autowater`. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Outcome* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from `bp.hit` and `bp.miss`. Since ignore trials are excluded from the dataset, only hit and miss remain.\n\nii.\n```python\nhit = np.array(bp['hit']).flatten().astype(bool)\nmiss = np.array(bp['miss']).flatten().astype(bool)\noutcome = hit[valid_trials].astype(np.int32)\n```\n\niii. Same raw variables as the reference, but the reference keeps ignore trials as a third class.\n\n## 6-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Outcome*?\n\ni. Hit trials are coded as correct (1), miss trials as incorrect (0). The AI has only 2 outcome classes (`['incorrect', 'correct']`). The reference has 3 classes (`['incorrect', 'correct', 'ignore']`) since it keeps ignore trials.\n\nii.\n```python\noutcome = hit[valid_trials].astype(np.int32)  # 0=incorrect (miss), 1=correct (hit)\n```\n\niii. The instructions specify \"incorrect, correct, ignore\" as the output values. The AI's 2-class approach omits the ignore class.\n\n## 7-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Tongue velocity* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from `obj.traj[1]` (bottom camera only), using the `top_tongue` feature. The AI uses only one camera view, whereas the reference combines both the side camera's `tongue` and the bottom camera's `top_tongue`.\n\nii.\n```python\ntraj_bottom = obj['traj'][1]  # bottom cam\ntongue_idx = None\nfor i, name in enumerate(feat_names):\n    if name == 'top_tongue':\n        tongue_idx = i\n        break\n```\n\niii. The AI's CONVERSION_NOTES says \"Tongue velocity: Use tip-of-tongue displacement from bottom cam view.\" The reference uses both cameras to recover more tongue visibility.\n\n## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Tongue velocity*?\n\ni. The AI computes x/y gradients multiplied by the video frame rate (400 Hz) to get velocity in pixels/second. No Gaussian smoothing of positions is applied before differentiation. No likelihood-based filtering is applied (it relies on NaN values already present in positions). The velocity is computed over ALL frames (including NaN transitions), then NaN bins are set to 0. No normalization is applied.\n\nii.\n```python\nvx_all = np.gradient(x) * VIDEO_FR\nvy_all = np.gradient(y) * VIDEO_FR\nspeed = np.sqrt(vx_all**2 + vy_all**2)\nspeed[~valid] = np.nan\n...\ntongue_vel = np.nan_to_num(tongue_vel, nan=0.0)\n```\n\niii. The reference applies a 5 ms Gaussian smooth to x,y positions, computes velocity only within contiguous runs of tracked frames (likelihood > 0.9), normalizes each camera view by its 90th percentile, averages the two views, and uses a \"not visible\" class for untracked bins. The AI's approach differs significantly: no smoothing, no run-based computation, no normalization, single camera, and NaN mapped to 0 instead of a separate class.\n\n## 7-c. How is `output` *Tongue velocity* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. The AI discretizes at the 50th percentile per session into 2 classes (0=low, 1=high). NaN values (tongue not visible) are set to 0 (low class) before discretization. The reference uses 3 classes with a separate \"not visible\" class (code 2).\n\nii.\n```python\ntongue_vel = np.nan_to_num(tongue_vel, nan=0.0)\ntongue_vel_disc = discretize_per_session(tongue_vel_valid)\n\ndef discretize_per_session(data_2d, percentile=50):\n    threshold = np.percentile(all_vals, percentile)\n    result = (data_2d >= threshold).astype(np.int32)\n    result[np.isnan(data_2d)] = 0\n    return result\n```\n\niii. The instructions specify: \"0: < 50th percentile, 1: >= 50th percentile, 2: not visible\". The AI maps untracked bins to 0 (low) instead of a separate class 2, and `output_values` lists only `['low', 'high']`. This means ~88% of tongue bins that should be \"not visible\" are incorrectly labeled as \"low\".\n\n## 7-d. How is `output` *Tongue velocity* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. How is `output` *Tongue velocity* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. The AI discretizes at the 50th percentile per session into 2 classes (0=low, 1=high). NaN values (tongue not visible) are set to 0 (low class) before discretization. The reference uses 3 classes with a separate \"not visible\" class (code 2).\n\nii.\n```python\ntongue_vel = np.nan_to_num(tongue_vel, nan=0.0)\ntongue_vel_disc = discretize_per_session(tongue_vel_valid)\n\ndef discretize_per_session(data_2d, percentile=50):\n    threshold = np.percentile(all_vals, percentile)\n    result = (data_2d >= threshold).astype(np.int32)\n    result[np.isnan(data_2d)] = 0\n    return result\n```\n\niii. The instructions specify: \"0: < 50th percentile, 1: >= 50th percentile, 2: not visible\". The AI maps untracked bins to 0 (low) instead of a separate class 2, and `output_values` lists only `['low', 'high']`. This means ~88% of tongue bins that should be \"not visible\" are incorrectly labeled as \"low\".\n\n## 7-d. How is `output` *Tongue velocity* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Paw velocity*?\n\ni. The AI fills NaN positions with nearest-neighbor interpolation, then computes x/y gradients multiplied by VIDEO_FR, and averages speeds across paw features. No Gaussian smoothing of positions.
